Instagram account which are accessible and target consumers within the
jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble Court. People can leave comments on videos,
posts and stories, and even interact with the creators of the videos, posts and
stories on any topic or subject of their choosing. As a result, the platform on
which these infringing videos/posts/stories are hosted are interactive in
nature, and a part of the cause of action qua Defendant No.1 has arisen in
New Delhi.
d. As the Defendants does not have any territorial limitation and can be used
to infringe the distinct MGD3 Trademarks of the Plaintiff within the
territorial jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble Court, if the same is not already done.
Further, the dynamic effect of violation of the Plaintiff‘s rights by the
Defendants is felt within the territorial limits of the Hon‘ble Court. On
account of the aforesaid, it is abundantly clear that a substantial and integral

238
part of the cause of action has also arisen within the territorial jurisdiction of
this Hon‘ble Court.
e. In accordance with the findings of the Hon‘ble Division Bench of the
Delhi High Court in the case of Tata Sons Pvt. Ltd. v. Hakunamatata Tata
Founders, FAO(OS) (COMM) 62/2022 dated 19th September, 2022 the fact
that third party interactive websites are unlawfully displaying the impugned
mark are accessible to customers in Delhi, and the fact the Defendants have
a looming presence on the virtual world in India, including the territorial
jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble court, gives this court the necessary territorial
jurisdiction, since this has the potentiality to cause deception and confusion
amongst consumers in Delhi.‖
408. In CS (COMM)-128/2026, the plaintiff, a US-based professional
association for human resource practitioners, has instituted the suit for trade
mark and copyright infringement along with a claim for passing off, in
respect of its ―SHRM‖ brand and the domain name ―www.shrm.org‖. The
plaintiff claims to be the registered proprietor of the marks ―SHRM‖,
―SHRM INDIA‖ and ―SOCIETY FOR HUMAN RESOURCE
MANAGEMENT‖ (device), together with copyright in the artistic work on its
website. The plaint alleges that defendant no. 1 has adopted the deceptively
similar domain name ―www.shrm.in‖, offering identical HR consultancy and
training services, thereby misappropriating the plaintiff‘s goodwill and
causing a likelihood of confusion. Defendant nos. 2 and 3, the domain name
registrar and the web host, have been impleaded as intermediaries.
409. Paras. 43 to 44 of the plaint contains the grounds on the basis of
which jurisdiction of this Court is sought to be invoked, it reads as under:
―43. That this Hon‘ble Court has the territorial jurisdiction to try and
adjudicate the present suit. The Defendant No. 1 is making false offerings
for the impugned services, displaying, and soliciting, have intention to
offer/solicit their impugned services and business under the impugned
Trademarks/Trade Name/Domain Names in New Delhi area through the
impugned website. In the era of virtualization and in light of the interactive

239
nature of the impugned websites being accessible and operational
everywhere, the impugned services under the impugned Trademarks/Trade
Name/Domain Name can be accessible within the jurisdiction of this
Hon‘ble Court. Thus, the Defendant No. 1 is committing the impugned acts
of infringement and passing-off in the New Delhi area through its impugned
website within the jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble Court by, soliciting, trading,
offering, and marketing in relation to the impugned services under the
impugned Trademark/Trade Name/Domain Name of the Plaintiff. Further
this Hon‘ble Court has the territorial jurisdiction to entertain and adjudicate
the present suit by virtue of Section 20(c) of the Code of Civil Procedure,
1908, as the cause of action has arisen within the territorial jurisdiction of
this Court. The impugned website/domain name adopted by Defendant No.
1, being www.shrm.in is accessible within the jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble
Court and the Defendant is soliciting business, offering services, and
causing confusion to consumers and stakeholders within the jurisdiction of
this Hon‘ble Court. Accordingly, the Plaintiff submits that this Hon‘ble
Court is vested with the requisite jurisdiction to try and decide the present
proceedings. Further, The Plaintiff also works for gain within the
jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble Court by organising its Annual Conferences in
New Delhi since 2011 and this year also the conference was held in New
Delhi on 13th and 14th November, 2025. The said event, being a significant
commercial and professional activity of the Plaintiff within the jurisdiction
of this Hon‘ble Court, confers jurisdiction upon this Court under Section
134 of the Trade Marks Act, 1999 as well as Section 62 of the Copyright
Act, 1957. The Plaintiff, therefore, has the right to institute and maintain the
present proceedings before this Hon‘ble Court, in addition to the jurisdiction
arising under Section 20(c) of the Code of Civil Procedure, 1908 and
Section 134 of the Trade Marks Act, 1999 as well as Section 62 of the
Copyright Act, 1957.
44. That due to the Defendant No.l‘s impugned activities and adoption of
such impugned domain name/website, the Plaintiff are suffering huge losses
both in business and in reputation and such losses are incapable of being
assessed in monetary terms. Unwary purchasers and consumers are being
deceived as to the origin of the said goods and business of the Plaintiff. The
Defendant No.1‘s gains are Plaintiff losses. The Plaintiff have no access to
the Defendant No. 1‘s accounts and the Defendant No. 1 is therefore liable
to render its accounts to the Plaintiff and to make good to the Plaintiff, the
profits and business earned by them. Without prejudice to the rendition of
accounts and in alternative thereto, the Defendant No. 1 is liable to pay
damages to the Plaintiff, which the Plaintiff conservatively and in a
restricted manner claims to the tune of Rs. 2,00,00,000/- (Rupees Two
crores only) even though the Plaintiff is entitled to much more.‖

240
410. In both CS (COMM)-874/2026 and CS (COMM)-128/2026
jurisdiction of this Court is sought to be invoked on grounds that the
defendant‘s websites, third party websites having the defendant‘s products
are accessible from Delhi, and the social media pages of the defendant can
also be accessed from Delhi. The test of Banyan Tree has not been satisfied
by the plaintiff.
411. The act of holding conferences and events in Delhi is also not an
essential part of the business; it cannot constitute carrying on of business.
The test laid down in Dhodha House is not satisfied. The plaintiff in
(COMM)-128/2026, thus, is also not carrying on business in Delhi.
412. In view of the foregoing reasons, in CS(COMM) 874/2026, territorial
jurisdiction of this Court is not made out under Section 20(a) or Section
20(c) of CPC, and Section 134(2) of the Trade Marks Act or Section 62(2)
of the Copyright Act. Similarly, in CS(COMM) 128/2026, the territorial
jurisdiction is not made out under Section 20(c) of CPC, and Section 134(2)
of the Trade Marks Act or Section 62(2) of the Copyright Act. Accordingly,
the plaints deserve to be returned to the plaintiffs, which may be presented
before the Court having the requisite territorial jurisdiction, if so advised.
Shiromani Gurudwara Prabandhak Committee & Anr. v. Galactic TV &
Communications Pvt. Ltd.
[CS(COMM)-883/2026]
413. Plaintiff no. 1 claims to be the authority managing and overseeing the
management and affairs of Sri Harmandir Sahib (Golden Temple). Plaintiff
no. 2 is stated to have the exclusive broadcasting rights to the Gurbani
Kirtan which takes place at the Golden Temple. The present suit is for

241
copyright infringement and unauthorised broadcast of the live and recorded
video stream of Gurbani Kirtan from the Golden Temple. It is alleged that
the defendants, without any licence, have re-transmitted the stream on their
channel ―GTC Punjabi‖ and on third-party platforms after adding their own
logo.
414. Paras. 65 to 67 of the plaint contains the grounds on the basis of
which jurisdiction of this Court is sought to be invoked, it reads as under:
―65. This Hon‘ble Court has the territorial jurisdiction to try and adjudicate
the present suit. The Code of the suit is 50000.01.
66. The Corporate Office (headquarters) of the Defendant No. 1 company,
from where the day-to-day business operations, executives, and
management actually work, is situated in Okhla Industrial Estate, Delhi.
67. Additionally, the unauthorised broadcasting and re-transmission of the
video stream (with the Defendants‘ logo) on third-party platforms (non-exhaustive) such as JioTV, YuppTV, KableOne, etc., which is the infringing
material, is accessible in Delhi - which has a big Sikh and Punjabi
community. Since the infringing content is accessible to the consumers
within the jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble Court, and additionally because the
damage is being caused to the Plaintiffs in Delhi as well, this Hon‘ble Court
would necessarily have the jurisdiction to try and adjudicate the present suit
under Section 62 of the Copyright Act read with Section 20 of the Code of
Civil Procedure.‖
415. The plaintiff has sought to invoke Section 20(a) of the CPC in order
to claim jurisdiction of this Court. It is undisputed that the defendant has a
sub-ordinate office at Delhi. It is also undisputed that a fraction of a cause of
action has accrued in Delhi owing to the impugned broadcasting of the
Gurbani Kirtan being accessible from Delhi. Cause of action has been
claimed to be the access by the public of the unauthorised broadcasting and

242
re-transmission of the video stream on third-party platforms such as JioTV,
YuppTV, KableOne etc.
416. As per the memo of parties plaintiff no. 1 is at Amritsar, Punjab.
Plaintiff no. 2 is at Mohali, Punjab. Defendant no. 1 has its registered office
at Mohali, Punjab. Defendant no. 2 has its registered office in London. The
plaintiff‘s own notice concerning the alleged infringement was addressed to
Defendant No. 1 at its Mohali office and the reply thereto was also issued
from the Mohali office. The dispute originates from the defendants
purportedly taking the live feed of the Gurbani from the plaintiff no. 1‘s
YouTube channel and broadcasting it on their channels.
417. The gravamen of this lis lies in the live feed emanating from the
Golden Temple and the Gurbani Kirtan that takes place at Amritsar, Punjab.
The cause of action available at Delhi, namely access to the broadcast, is
also available at Amritsar Punjab. It is indeed available in most if not all
parts of India. The material, integral, and essential part of the cause of
action, however, has arisen in Amritsar. The mere fact that access to the
infringing material is taking place in Delhi and the defendant has a sub-ordinate office at Delhi183 does not make Delhi the forum conveniens. The
courts at Amritsar are ex facie the forum conveniens to entertain this lis.
418. Accordingly, the plaint deserves to be returned to the plaintiff, which
may be presented before the Court having the requisite territorial
jurisdiction, if so advised.

183 As per Sanjay Dalia, para. 16, the principal office is to be registered office.

243
Sanjay Kumar Jain and Anr. v. Nandi Ayurved and Ors.
[CS(COMM)-746/2026]
419. Both the plaintiffs are in Rajasthan. Defendant nos. 1, 2 and 4 are in
Rajasthan. Defendant no. 3 is in Punjab. Defendant no. 5, IndiaMart, is in
Delhi. Thus, all of the contesting defendants except one are in Rajasthan.
420. The suit has been filed for trademark and copyright infringement
along with a claim for passing off. It is contended that the plaintiffs are the
registered owners of several trademarks including ―ACHAL CHANCHAL‖,
―ACHAL‖, ―TEJ PRAKASH‖, ―RADHA PRAKASH‖, ―KACHAL‖ and
other ACHAL formative marks. The plaint narrates that the defendants
allegedly have dishonestly adopted and commenced use of the marks
―CHANCHAL‖ and ―TEJ PRAKASH‖ in relation to Ayurvedic medicinal
preparations.
421. Paras. 71 to 77 of the plaint contains the grounds on the basis of
which jurisdiction of this Court is sought to be invoked, it reads as under:
―71. That the Defendants are actively offering for sale, advertising and
promoting their infringing products bearing the impugned marks through
various online platforms and social media, including their Instagram page,
which are accessible across India, including within the territorial jurisdiction
of this Hon‘ble Court. The said infringing products are available for
purchase by consumers through such online platforms and can be delivered
to customers within the territorial jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble Court. The
Defendants are therefore purposefully targeting consumers within the
jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble Court, and the impugned goods are being
marketed and offered for sale to consumers in Delhi. Consequently, a part of
the cause of action has arisen within the territorial jurisdiction of this
Hon‘ble Court, and this Hon‘ble Court has the jurisdiction to entertain and
try the present suit.
72. The Defendants are engaged in systematic and purposeful commercial
activities directed towards consumers located within Delhi. The Defendants

244
have advertised, offered for sale and sold the impugned products through e-commerce platforms including Defendant No. 5, IndiaMART Intermesh
Ltd. which facilitates transactions, listings, communications of the
infringing products to purchasers located within the territorial jurisdiction of
this Hon‘ble Court.
73. Defendant No. 4, Ramesh Belt House & Mayur Puja Bhandar, in active
collusion with Defendant Nos. 1 to 3, has been fulfilling orders of the
impugned goods has caused delivery of the infringing products to Delhi
courier. The evidence of delivery of the infringing goods to addresses
located within Delhi gives rise to a direct commercial effect within the
territorial jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble Court.
74. The Defendants‘ online listings and advertisements are interactive in
nature, inviting orders from and enabling direct transactions with consumers
in Delhi. Accordingly, a part of the cause of action has clearly arisen within
this Hon‘ble Court‘s jurisdiction.
75. That this Hon‘ble Court has the territorial jurisdiction to entertain and
try the present suit under the provisions of Section 134 of the Trade Marks
Act, 1999 and Section 62 of the Copyright Act, 1957, as well as under the
provisions of the Code of Civil Procedure, 1908.
76. That the Plaintiffs carry on business and have commercial presence
within the territorial jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble Court through their
distribution network, sale of products through dealers, stockists, chemists,
pharmacies and online platforms. The Plaintiff‘s products bearing the
Plaintiff‘s Marks are widely sold and available to consumers within the
jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble Court.
77. That the Defendants are actively offering for sale, advertising and
promoting their infringing products bearing the impugned marks
―CHANCHAL‖ and ―TEJ PRAKASH‖ through online platforms and social
media, including their Instagram page, which are accessible across India
including within the territorial jurisdiction of this Hon‘ble Court. The said
products can be purchased by consumers within the jurisdiction of this
Hon‘ble Court and are capable of being delivered within Delhi.‖
422. Through the mere listing, which is contended to be an offer for sale,
advertisement and promotion of the impugned product on websites,
jurisdiction cannot be established as it falls foul of the requirements of
Banyan Tree. It is pleaded that certain goods were delivered in Delhi. No

245
document in support of the averment has been attached in the plaint. In the
absence of supporting documents, the averment remains an empty and
hollow statement made to confer jurisdiction where there is none. In any
case, in arguendo, if it is assumed that there is a fraction of cause of action
which has accrued within the jurisdiction of this Court, that, in itself ought
not to be the reason to entertain the present suit. A purported delivery at
Delhi could well have happened at Rajasthan or for instance at Jhumri
Telaiya. This fact which is dependent upon the wilful conduct of the
plaintiff, ought not to make this Court the forum conveniens to adjudicate
this suit. Thus for reasons further elaborated in the analysis of CS(COMM)
1081/2026, this court is not forum conveniens to entertain this lis.
VI. ORDER
423. Each of the plaints discussed above, deserve to be returned on
grounds of territorial jurisdiction. Consequently, the plaints in CS (COMM)-
987/2026, CS (COMM)-996/2026, CS (COMM)-611/2026, CS (COMM)-
980/2026, CS (COMM)-1081/2026, CS (COMM)-1010/2026, CS (COMM)-
917/2026, CS (COMM)-444/2026, CS (COMM)-1115/2026, CS (COMM)-
1041/2026, CS (COMM)-1120/2026, CS (COMM)-598/2022, CS (COMM)-
111/2026, CS (COMM)-874/2026, CS (COMM)-128/2026, CS (COMM)-
883/2026 and CS (COMM)-746/2026, are returned to the plaintiff under
Order VII Rule 10 read with Section 151 of the CPC, to be presented before
an appropriate court, if so advised. Pending applications if any stand
disposed of.

246
424. Liberty is, however, granted to each of the plaintiffs in the above-mentioned suits to make an application, and avail appropriate directions,
under Order VII Rule 10A of the CPC.
(PURUSHAINDRA KUMAR KAURAV)
 JUDGE
NOVEMBER 03, 2026
Rao
